# Gold-Set Drift Log

Chronological log of convention-validation findings AND CONVENTIONS-edit events. Populated at the item-25 convention-validation checkpoint and at every subsequent every-25-items drift check (Type A), and at the time of CONVENTIONS.md edits triggered by annotation work (Type B).

Entries come in two types. Use the template matching the entry's purpose; do not mix the formats in a single entry.

---

## Entry Type A — Checkpoint findings

Used at item-25 convention-validation checkpoints (§8.1) and every-25-items drift checks (§8.2). One entry per checkpoint. Records drift findings, pattern-watch observations, and (at §8.1 only) edit-velocity review.

Format:

```
## YYYY-MM-DD — <item-N checkpoint | every-25 drift check>

- Finding: <description; link to file:line if possible>
- Action: <in-place correction | parking-lot escalation | CONVENTIONS edit | none>
- Pattern watch: <note if this is the Nth recurrence of a prior finding>
- Edit velocity (§8.1 only): <count and trend observation>
```

## Entry Type B — CONVENTIONS-edit event OR pattern watch

Used in two cases, both using the same pointer format:

- **CONVENTIONS-edit subtype.** A CONVENTIONS.md edit was made in response to annotation work. One entry per edit event (an "event" is a single batch of coordinated edits, not necessarily a single section change). Middle field = §-section refs.
- **Pattern-watch subtype.** An annotation surfaced a pattern considered for PL promotion but resisted (typically at n=1 or n=2, below the §7.1 ≥3-instance threshold), with a pre-registered re-evaluation trigger. Two middle-field subtypes per CONVENTIONS §8.0 step 3:
  - `pattern-watch — <pattern-name>` — registration entry; records the pattern and sets the trigger condition; does NOT count toward trigger.
  - `observation — <pattern-name>` — additional instance entry; counts toward trigger.
  
  Scanned at every checkpoint per `CONVENTIONS.md` §8.0 so trigger-firing is detected passively rather than by annotator memory. Re-evaluation triggers should take an explicit form such as "if ≥N additional cases by item M, promote to PL."

Pointer-style — records *what happened and why*, not the content of the change itself; canonical detail lives in the §3.2 row `Triggered by:` line, the git diff, or the annotation's notes field.

Format:

```
YYYY-MM-DD | <§-section-refs | pattern-watch — PATTERN_NAME | observation — PATTERN_NAME> | triggered by <item-id or description> | <one-line summary>
```

See `CONVENTIONS.md` §8 for checkpoint procedures (including §8.0 for the pattern-watch trigger scan with the canonical grep recipe) and §3.2 for parking-lot pattern rows.

2026-04-22 | pattern-watch — attribution-scope-not-referent | triggered by claim-heidegger-bcap-079 | when an exegetical claim attributes a view to a collective or authority source ("For the Greeks...", "According to Aristotle...", "Plato holds that..."), the attribution source functions as scope-marking for the claim's content rather than as a content-referent. Analogous to how a "for-X" clause in English specifies the interpretive frame without being a referent of the claim's content itself. Criterion: the attribution source is NOT a referent if its role is purely to establish whose view is being reported; it IS a referent if the claim's content is ABOUT that source's activity (e.g., "The Greeks developed λόγος through rhetorical practice" — Greeks is a referent because the claim is about Greeks' activity, not merely attributing a view to them). Re-evaluation: at item-25 convention-validation checkpoint; promote to PL code if ≥3 instances observed (this registration case + 2 additional). Expected recurrence loci: secondary-literature items attributing views to historical figures, Heidegger's exegetical claims across BCAP, philosophy-of-mind items citing contemporary authors.

2026-04-22 | pattern-watch — context-sensitive-canonicalization | triggered by items 091 and 079 | same surface term canonicalizes to different rich nodes across claims based on argumentative context: "speaking" canonicalizes to Discourse in 091 (Heideggerian-assertive-about-Dasein) but to Logos in 079 (exegetical-about-Greeks). The argumentative mode (assertive vs exegetical) drives the target, not the surface word. Criterion: when a thin slug with ambiguous scope (e.g., 'speaking') maps to different rich canonicals depending on whether the claim is asserting a framework-native view or interpreting a historical position, flag for context-sensitivity. Re-evaluation: ≥2 more cases by item 20 where the same surface term canonicalizes to different rich nodes across claims based on argumentative context. If trigger fires, item-25 checkpoint has real signal about how to document context-sensitive canonicalization in §3. If trigger does not fire, pattern-watch reads as "tried to codify at n=2, evidence didn't accumulate" — useful negative evidence too. Back-sweep scope if promoted: items 091 (Discourse) and 079 (Logos), both would gain a subtype-tag or flag indicating argumentative mode.

This entry now serves as the 036 retrospective observation for the registered pattern.] Third consecutive accept of technical-upgrade on primary_heidegger material at the time of original logging (091 speaking→Discourse accepted as rich-preferred default; 079 speaking→Logos accepted per exegetical-context canonicalization; 036 visible→Disclosedness initially presented as Option A/B judgment, upgraded-on-passage-review to Option A accept after surrounding-passage evidence confirmed hermeneutic-disclosure reading). 2026-04-22 | pattern-watch — heidegger-technical-upgrade-default | triggered by claim-heidegger-bcap-066 (fourth consecutive accept) | thin English slugs denoting ordinary concepts that admit technical Heideggerian readings default to rich Heideggerian canonicals on primary_heidegger material when surrounding context establishes technical deployment. Observed instances (n=4 pre-populated via retrospective observation entries + 036 rename): 091 (speaking→Discourse), 079 (speaking→Logos), 036 (visible→Disclosedness, upgraded-on-review), 066 (essential→Essence). All four acceptances have been on BCAP; rule's scope for non-BCAP Heidegger untested. **Re-evaluation triggers (either fires forced evaluation):** (1) first GENUINE REJECTION of a technical-upgrade candidate on primary_heidegger material surfaces an edge case and forces scope refinement; (2) item-25 convention-validation checkpoint with accumulated data. **Genuine-rejection definition:** a case is a genuine rejection only if (a) on a primary_heidegger claim, (b) a thin English slug + rich Heideggerian canonical pair is available for the same concept, (c) the surrounding context establishes technical deployment, AND (d) the final label is nonetheless the thin slug. Cases where the thin slug is labeled because the surrounding context does NOT establish technical deployment are NOT rejections — they are correct applications of the rule's precondition (the rule's preconditions are not met, which is different from the rule being overruled on evidence). This distinction prevents the retrospective-ambiguity failure mode the §8.0 canonical-recipe discipline was designed to address. **Candidate §3.0 codification at re-evaluation:** "On primary_heidegger material, thin English slugs denoting ordinary concepts that admit technical Heideggerian readings default to the rich Heideggerian canonical when surrounding context establishes technical deployment; underreading is the failure mode, not overreading." **Scope question to resolve at codification:** whether the rule applies to all primary_heidegger material or specifically to Heidegger-on-Aristotle (BCAP-style) material — all four observed instances are from BCAP, so non-BCAP generalization is untested.

2026-04-22 | observation — s32-cross-author-concept-collision-row-scope-refinement | triggered by 115 (refuting-mode case) | §3.2 cross-author-concept-collision row scope analysis from first-instance activation: the row's expected-trigger covers TWO structurally distinct sub-modes that warrant separate consideration at item-25 codification. (1) **Porting-mode** — author A introduces a term, author B uses it to refer to the same concept (e.g., 031 Audi uses Kim's `explanatory relation` straightforwardly; the technical sense is shared). The collision is at the ontology-curation level (slugs not merged) but not at the concept-divergence level (concept is shared). (2) **Refuting-mode** — author B uses author A's term while critiquing author A's view (115 McDonnell-Wildman use Chalmers's `virtual digitalism` while refuting Chalmers's view). The collision is at BOTH ontology-curation AND concept-divergence levels (technical sense actually diverges: McDonnell-Wildman's `virtual digitalism` in refutes-stance has dialectical-target meaning, Chalmers's has positive-view meaning). The §3.2 row text currently doesn't distinguish; item-25 codification candidate: refine row to specify the two sub-modes, possibly with distinct PL/AF codes per sub-mode or merged with subtype-tag in concept slot (parallel to AF5-subtype-proliferation candidate (ii) AF5:opinion(elided-contrast) form). 2026-04-28 | observation — dev-holdout-contamination | triggered by Step 4 dev-ordering computation | 5 holdout claim_ids found in dev candidate pool (4 aristotle: 033/037/053/058; 1 heidegger: 036). Fix applied at ordering-computation time via exclusion filter inside `scripts/compute-dev-ordering.py` (pool 150→145); disjointness verified post-fix (overlap=0). **Cause resolved via pre-Unit-C diagnostic:** all 5 contaminated items appear in BOTH the `dev-*` AND `holdout-*` candidate sample files; the candidate sampler (`scripts/sample-gold-candidates.py`) did not enforce dev/holdout pool disjointness at sampling time. Broader: full holdout-pool ↔ dev-pool intersection is 15 items, not just the 5 — but only 5 ended up in final holdout (the other 10 are "almost-holdout-but-not-selected" candidates that remain in dev pool and are legitimately eligible for dev sampling since they are not in final holdout). **Diagnostic completed pre-Unit-C** (per user direction): (a) cross-genre overlap WITHIN dev pool = 0; (b) cross-genre overlap WITHIN holdout pool = 0; (c) internal duplicates within any candidate file = 0; (d) final holdout ⊆ candidate holdout pool ✓ (no out-of-pool items in final holdout). Localized failure: only dev/holdout boundary affected. Final-holdout exclusion filter sufficient — broader cleanup not required. **Distinct from the source-record-schema-variability cluster** (036 empty-nearby_provenance, 091 Bekker-on-Heidegger, 049 empty-quote-with-ground-field) — that pattern is schema-variability the §5.4 procedure handles; this is pool-membership integrity, structurally different. **Item-25 codification candidate:** corpus-data-quality §3 sub-section addressing pool-membership integrity (sampler-disjointness-enforcement, internal-duplicate-prevention, final-set-subset-of-pool verification) beyond schema variability. Forward defense: any future holdout extension or candidate re-sampling must re-run the exclusion filter and re-verify post-fix disjointness; the lock-against-silent-change provision in `dev-ordering.md` addenda is the governance hook. **Forward note (upstream-vs-downstream):** the sampler defect persists at `scripts/sample-gold-candidates.py`; the current downstream filter (in `compute-dev-ordering.py`) is sufficient for THIS corpus generation but any future re-sampling will reproduce the contamination unless the sampler is patched. Pre-condition for any re-sampling work: patch sampler to enforce pool disjointness (dev-pool ∩ holdout-pool = ∅ at sampling time) before re-running. Do not trust the downstream filter as a complete fix — it is a contamination-tolerant downstream control, not a contamination-prevention upstream fix.

2026-04-28 | observation — s84-clarification-candidate-cluster | triggered by dev-5 close-out review of accumulating §8.4 governance concerns | [UPDATED 2026-04-28 Remediation Phase 3: cluster expanded from 3 to 5 cases with addition of (4) retraction-protocol middle-field tokenization (provisional protocol used in this Remediation; first retraction event in project) + (5) amendment-vs-retraction-boundary (when does [UPDATED] inline-appendix suffice vs. when does middle-field re-tokenization apply; AF5-subtype-proliferation [UPDATED] precedent vs. Remediation 2026-04-28 RETRACTED- middle-field precedent).] Original three concerns clustering around one underlying question: how does §8.4 / §8.0 handle entries whose form was retrofitted to fit existing Type A/Type B types rather than created cleanly under them? **(1) Locked-plan-driven interim checks** (this turn's dev-5 interim check entry: Type A by template-match with trigger = locked plan Decision 8 rather than §8.1/§8.2 cadence) — non-canonical trigger source. **(2) Registration-prose-as-evidence vs recipe-honesty** (AF5-subtype-proliferation bracketed UPDATEs in registration entries; pre-fix implicit-and-missing case where registration prose enumerated cases that the canonical-recipe grep `\| observation — \|` couldn't see) — instance-evidence folded into prose where §8.0 recipes can't count it. **(3) Registration-vs-observation count distinction** (recipe greps `\| observation — \|` only; registration excluded from count by design; surfaced as a count-table discrepancy in dev-5 close-out where the conversation prose miscounted by including registration as instance — drift-log entries themselves were correct, only the conversation report needed correction). **(4) Retraction protocol** (Remediation 2026-04-28 first retraction event; provisional middle-field tokenization `RETRACTED-{observation,pattern-watch} —` used; original recipe greps continue to work without amendment; retracted entries excluded by middle-field non-match). **(5) Amendment-vs-retraction-boundary** (when does [UPDATED] inline-appendix pattern suffice vs. when does middle-field re-tokenization apply; AF5-subtype-proliferation [UPDATED] entries changed instance count from 3 to 7 without re-tokenization; Remediation 2026-04-28 retractions used re-tokenization for substantive basis-failure rather than count-shading; the boundary criteria need explicit specification). The retrofit pressure is structural: §8.4 has only two types (Type A checkpoint findings; Type B CONVENTIONS-edit OR pattern-watch) when reality has more shapes, and creating a clean third type would require CONVENTIONS edit (counter increment, etc.). **Item-25 §8.4 clarification candidate (option (c) per dev-5 close-out):** legitimize the retrofit pattern via explicit `trigger=<source-class>` markers in middle-field for entries matching existing template-shapes but with non-canonical trigger sources. Markers follow fixed enumeration to enable §8.0 grepping. Source classes identified so far: `trigger=locked-plan-decision-N` (interim checks driven by locked plan rather than §8.1/§8.2 cadence); `trigger=retroactive-instance` (observations added post-hoc to align recipe count with actual instance count, e.g., implicit-and-missing fix); `trigger=remediation-retraction` (RETRACTED- middle-field tokenization for first-retraction event Remediation 2026-04-28). Enumeration extensible via §8.4 amendment as new classes surface. Cross-reference: dev-3 + dev-4 + dev-5 close-out conversations contain the specific cases motivating concerns 1-3; Remediation 2026-04-28 (data/gold/protocols/remediation-2026-04-28.md) contains concerns 4-5.

2026-04-28 | observation — operative-source-ambiguity | triggered by dev-6 retroactive ontology-source verification | The operative ontology source is data/corpus/index/compiled-index.json (7,389 nodes per README §1 + retrieval recipe at line 138). The 277-node data/corpus/index/ontology-embeddings.jsonl is a subset for embedding-based fuzzy matching, not the operative ontology. Dev-3, dev-4, dev-5 annotations were authored against the 277-node subset rather than the 7,389-node compiled-index, producing false-AF4 calls and derived pattern-watch errors (see Remediation 2026-04-28 §2.3-§2.5 + drift-log RETRACTED- entries above). Root cause: README's "ontology IS 7,389 nodes; most authoring will reference the ~200 most-connected nodes" framing creates ambiguity between subset-as-default vs. full-ontology-with-rich-preferred reading; the 277-node file's name "ontology-embeddings.jsonl" lacks an explicit "subset" qualifier; dev-1/dev-2 happened to label correctly via the rich-preferred discipline that doesn't depend on the subset/full distinction. Reading C governs (full 7,389 operative; rich-preferred when both rich and thin concept-match per §3.0 + protocol Tests 1-3; thin labeled when no rich passes) — confirmed via holdout cross-check (56/56 holdout labels in 7,389-node set; 12/56 in 277-embedded). Corrected ontology-source documented in data/gold/protocols/candidate-enumeration-protocol-v1.md §1.1; six-check protocol governs candidate enumeration going forward. Pure historical record; forward-looking fix recommendations in separate observation — corpus-data-quality-recommendations entry below.

2026-04-28 | observation — wrong-context-rich-canonical-claim-text-vs-chapter-context-distinction | triggered by claim-heidegger-bcap-012 | first instance where claim_text's own-face framework deployment differs from chapter's argumentative deployment. 2026-04-29 | pattern-watch — load-bearing-modifier-without-ontology-coverage (extension) | triggered by claim-aristotle-da-3.3-034 (dev-10 "partly") | extends pattern-watch from n=3 (dev-3 + dev-9×2) to n=5. Verified absent from compiled-index: `partly` (adverb), `partial` (adjective); closest cousins `part` (too generic, peripheral, "Concept introduced via Aristotle"), `partial cause`/`partial explanation`/`partial ground` (peripheral compounds about partial-causation, not quantifier-distribution). "Partly" load-bearing at dialectical-transition position: claim_text's "partly of imagination and partly of judgment" IS the simple-decomposition view Aristotle pivots from to the richer enumeration in the immediately following sentences ("the faculties in virtue of which we do this are sense, opinion, knowledge, thought"); "partly" encodes the partial-decomposition reading that licenses Aristotle's downstream refinement. Cumulative count n=5 across 3 dev items: dev-3 "characteristic" head-noun-not-in-ontology; dev-9 "active" mode-modifier on `perception`; dev-9 "unbridgeable" modifier on `causal gap`; dev-10 "partly" quantifier-distributor on `imagination`/`judgment`. §7.1 promotion held — n=5 is robust evidence but pre-item-25 promotion would add a third codification draft to item-25 (alongside load-bearing-modifier formalization + slug-graveyard-split), reverting the codification-load-shift dev-5's 5th-mechanism early-trigger reverted from. Codification candidate flagged for item-25 evaluation alongside other §7.1 candidates; deferred unless n grows substantially (e.g., n≥8 by dev-25 forces earlier codification).

2026-04-29 | observation — PL-paraphrase-distortion-fires-three-mechanisms | triggered by claim-frede-1992-030 (dev-14) | second dev-set §5.4 step 4 PL:paraphrase-distortion fire (after dev-4 fodor-1974-021; first since then). Source quote at 428b25-30 vs paraphrase shows THREE distinct distortion mechanisms: (1) STRENGTH-ESCALATION — quote 'does not give any further depiction' (some-depiction-given implication) → paraphrase 'fails to provide an account' (none-given implication); confirmatory mechanism; alone may not fire step 4 (degree-of-claim issue). (2) REFERENT-SHIFT — paraphrase's `account` (Frede-introduced peripheral) replaces quote's `depiction` (verified absent from compiled-index); genuinely new content; DISPOSITIVE mechanism that drives step 4 fire on independent grounds. (3) SELECTIVE-REPRESENTATION — paraphrase drops second critique in source paragraph ('nor, even more deplorably, [phenomenological description distinguishing phantasiai from sense-perceptions]') despite Frede's elevation hedge; paraphrase-as-selection rather than paraphrase-as-modification; confirmatory mechanism; alone likely doesn't fire step 4. Combined: three mechanisms make step 4 fire decisively. Item-25 §5.4 step 4 trigger-criteria refinement candidate: distinguish dispositive (referent-shift) vs confirmatory (strength-escalation, selective-representation) mechanisms — paired with drift-log Entry C sub-pattern record.
